Propositions 1 through 15 do not directly address the bending or deformation of an object, which is the core of the contradiction we're analyzing. Proposition 15 does talk about an object being bent and no longer in its original shape, but it doesn't relate to the use of the Shichishito in the crime.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

None of the propositions directly apply to the contradiction identified.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction arises from the spatial reasoning that if the Shichishito was used to strike Atmey, it would likely have been bent or deformed due to the force applied. However, the evidence clearly states that the Shichishito was not bent, indicating that it was not used in such a way, thus contradicting Atmey's testimony.

This implies that the Taurusaurus costume contained President Wang's body. However, Evidence 5, the Crime Scene Notes, mentions that the body was lying face-up on top of the dirt, and there's no dirt on the front of the body. This suggests that the body was directly placed on the ground without being inside any object that could have shielded it from the dirt.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions can help us identify a contradiction based on the spatial relationships described.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

This proposition aligns with Lang's testimony that the body was hidden inside the costume. However, this does not directly address the contradiction regarding the presence or absence of dirt on the body.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

None of the spatial propositions directly address the contradiction between the body being inside the costume and the lack of dirt on the front of the body. The contradiction arises from the spatial relationship between the body and the dirt. If the body was inside the costume, it would likely come into contact with the dirt inside the costume, leading to dirt on the body. However, the evidence notes that there is no dirt on the front of the body, suggesting that the body was not inside the costume but directly placed on the ground.

However, Evidence 6 (Circus Map) indicates that Acro is on the third floor, and there is no direct mention of his room being obstructed or him being physically restrained in a way that would prevent him from seeing out of the window.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 14 states: "If the Wooden Box is inside the cafeteria and Acro is facing the window, then Acro cannot see the Wooden Box if the window is blocked." Similarly, Proposition 15 states: "If the Wooden Box is inside the cafeteria and Acro is facing the window, then Acro cannot see the Ringmaster's head if the window is blocked."

These propositions imply that the inability to see out of the window due to it being blocked could explain Acro's claim in Testimony 2. However, the story does not provide information about whether the window was blocked or not.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

According to the map, Acro is on the third floor, and there is no indication that his room was obstructed in such a way that he could not see out of the window. Therefore, the contradiction lies in the fact that Acro's claim of being unable to see out of the window while lifting the bust is not supported by the spatial layout described in the map.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

None of the listed propositions directly address the contradiction about the relative positions of Terry and Valerie. However, the evidence suggests that the shack is a 5-minute walk from Eagle Bridge, and the weather data indicates that it was snowing until around 10:50 PM, which could potentially obscure visibility.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to look at the spatial propositions that involve the shack and Dusky Bridge. Propositions 1, 2, 3, and 5 are relevant.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

- **Proposition 1:** If Butz was in the shack at 10:30 PM, then the shack is far from Dusky Bridge.
- **Proposition 2:** If Dusky Bridge is on fire at 10:30 PM, then the fire is visible from the shack.
- **Proposition 3:** If Butz saw Dusky Bridge on fire from the shack, then the shack is in a position where it can see Dusky Bridge.
- **Proposition 5:** If Butz arrived at Dusky Bridge and Nick showed up less than a minute later, then Butz and Nick are in the same location at approximately 10:30 PM.

Butz's testimonies (Testimony 0 and Testimony 2) suggest that he was able to see Dusky Bridge from the shack, which aligns with Proposition 3. However, Proposition 1 states that the shack is far from Dusky Bridge, which contradicts the idea that Butz could clearly see the fire from the shack.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Proposition 1 and Proposition 3 directly conflict. Proposition 1 suggests that the shack is far from Dusky Bridge, while Proposition 3 implies that the shack has a clear view of Dusky Bridge. Since Butz claims he saw the fire from the shack, these two propositions cannot both be true simultaneously.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Proposition 14 does apply and helps us identify the contradiction. The bullet hitting the back of the safe implies that the bullet traveled in a direction away from Wocky Kitaki, contrary to Stickler's testimony that the bullet hit Pal Meraktis from the front.

**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, Ema states that the victim (Dumas Gloomsbury) was hit from behind and fell into the lantern. This implies that the force of the blow came from behind Gloomsbury, causing him to fall forward into the lantern. 

However, Evidence 6, the Photo of Victim, shows that there are red petals on top of Gloomsbury and a light bulb close to him. Additionally, there is a piece of paper near his right hand. These details suggest that the environment around the victim was such that items were positioned above or near him, which could imply different scenarios depending on how he fell into the lantern.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions can help us understand the contradiction better. Specifically, we look at Propositions related to positioning above/below or in front/behind.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 6: If the red petals are on top of Gloomsbury, then Gloomsbury is below the red petals.
This proposition directly applies to the scenario described in the photo. According to the photo, the red petals are on top of Gloomsbury, meaning he is below them. However, if he was hit from behind and fell into the lantern, it would be unusual for red petals to be on top of him unless he was somehow flipped over after falling into the lantern.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Proposition 6 does apply and helps us identify the contradiction. The spatial arrangement implied by the photo (red petals on top of Gloomsbury) contradicts the testimony that he was hit from behind and fell into the lantern. If he was hit from behind and fell into the lantern, it would be more likely for the petals to be scattered around him rather than neatly on top of him.

## OBJECTIVE:
**FIND THE CONTRADICTION:** Which evidence and testimony contradict each other?

## CONSTRAINT:
**You must only answer ONE PAIR.**

## ANALYSIS FRAMEWORK:
Please follow this format:

**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

---

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:
**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, followed by nothing else.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, we understand that Gumshoe was standing guard in front of Lobby No. 2 and did not move from the Lobby No. 2 door until he heard the gunshot. However, Evidence 11 states that Gumshoe's fingerprints were found on the hallway bench, suggesting that he was at least momentarily near or touching the bench.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if there are any spatial propositions that relate to the positions of Gumshoe relative to the Lobby No. 2 door and the hallway bench.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Propositions 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 13, and 15 are relevant to the spatial relationships described. Specifically, Proposition 5 (near) and Proposition 15 (out of reach) are pertinent.

- Proposition 5: If Gumshoe is near to the Lobby No. 2 door then the Lobby No. 2 door is near to Gumshoe.
- Proposition 15: If Gumshoe is out of reach of the hallway bench then Gumshoe cannot touch the hallway bench.

Gumshoe's testimony suggests he was always near the Lobby No. 2 door and did not move away from it. However, his fingerprints on the hallway bench suggest he was within reach and could touch the bench, which contradicts his claim of not moving from the Lobby No. 2 door.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, the spatial propositions do apply. The contradiction arises because if Gumshoe was always near the Lobby No. 2 door and did not move, he would have been out of reach of the hallway bench, according to his testimony. However, the presence of his fingerprints on the bench indicates he was within reach and touched it, which contradicts his statement.

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

{"evidence": 11, "testimony": 3}

  "evidence": 11,
  "testimony": 3

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, Nash and Ringo Skulkin claim that they were inside the pawnbrokery when they heard the gunshot and that they were frightened by it, thinking the shooter would come out and get them next. They also mention that they put everything back where they found it and left the premises. 

On the other hand, Evidence 9 (Autopsy Report) and Evidence 10 (Crime Scene Photograph) indicate that the victim, Mr Windibank, was shot from behind and found dead in the storeroom. This suggests that the shooter was positioned behind Windibank, likely in the storeroom or another location from where a shot could travel through the wall to hit Windibank in the back.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions can help us identify a contradiction based on the spatial relationships described in the testimonies and the evidences.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Propositions 2, 3, 8, 12, and 14 are relevant here. Proposition 2 states that if the Skulkin brothers are inside the pawnbrokery and the gunshot occurs, they are disconnected from the outside. Proposition 3 indicates that if the gunshot is heard by the Skulkin brothers and they are in the main shop, the gunshot must originate from the rear storeroom. Proposition 8 says that if the gunshot is heard and the Skulkin brothers are in the main shop, then the Skulkin brothers are not in the same room as the shooter. Proposition 12 states that if the gunshot is fired and the Skulkin brothers are in the main shop, then the Skulkin brothers cannot see the shooter if the wall is solid. Proposition 14 says that if the gunshot is fired and the Skulkin brothers are in the main shop, then the Skulkin brothers are not in the same room as the victim.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

Based on the spatial propositions, particularly Prop 8 and Prop 14, we can infer that if the Skulkin brothers were in the main shop when the gunshot occurred, they would not be in the same room as the shooter or the victim. However, the testimonies suggest that the Skulkin brothers were inside the pawnbrokery when the gunshot happened, and they were frightened by it, implying they might have been in the same room as the shooter or at least close enough to hear the gunshot directly without it traveling through a wall. This contradicts the spatial propositions indicating that the gunshot originated from the storeroom and the Skulkin brothers were in the main shop.

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, we know that Shih-na was inside Babahl's office during the first fire (Testimony 0) and was searching for the Yatagarasu in the next room over during the second fire (Testimony 1). Additionally, she heard a scream from the next room over (Testimony 2) and was unable to see the Yatagarasu despite being in the next room (Testimony 3).

From the evidences, we have the Revolving Fireplace Wall (Evidence 29), which indicates that the Yatagarasu escaped through the wall connecting Babahl's office to the next room. This suggests that the Yatagarasu could potentially move between these two rooms.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions apply to the situation described by Shih-na's testimonies and the Revolving Fireplace Wall evidence.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Propositions 1, 3, 4, and 11 are relevant to our analysis:
- Prop 1 states that if Shih-na is inside Babahl's office during the first fire, she is disconnected from Allebahst's office.
- Prop 3 states that if Shih-na hears a scream from the next room over, she is disconnected from the source of the scream.
- Prop 4 states that if Shih-na is in the next room and unable to see the Yatagarasu, then the Yatagarasu is blocked from Shih-na's view.
- Prop 11 states that if the Yatagarasu is inside Babahl's office and the office is connected to Allebahst's office via the revolving fireplace wall, then the Yatagarasu can potentially enter Allebahst's office.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

Based on Proposition 11, if the Yatagarasu is inside Babahl's office and the office is connected to Allebahst's office via the Revolving Fireplace Wall, then the Yatagarasu can potentially enter Allebahst's office. However, according to Shih-na's testimony (Testimony 3), she was in the next room and unable to see the Yatagarasu, implying that the Yatagarasu was blocked from her view. This contradicts the idea that the Yatagarasu could freely move between the offices, as suggested by the Revolving Fireplace Wall evidence (Evidence 29).
